```python
        graph = [[] for _ in range(n_circles)]
        
        for i in range(n_circles):

                dist = get_distance_2d(x1, y1, x2, y2)
                if dist <= r1 + r2:
                    graph[i].append(j)
                    graph[j].append(i)
        
        # Count connected components using DFS
        visited = [False] * n_circles
        components = 0
        
        for i in range(n_circles):
            if not visited[i]:
                components += 1
                # DFS
                stack = [i]
                while stack:
                    node = stack.pop()
                    if visited[node]:
                        continue
                    visited[node] = True
                    for neighbor in graph[node]:
                        if not visited[neighbor]:
                            stack.append(neighbor)
        
        states.append(components)
    
    # Now detect transitions
    transitions = []
    for i in range(1, len(states)):
        if states[i] > states[i-1]:
            transitions.append('1')
        else:
            transitions.append('0')
    
    return len(transitions), ''.join(transitions)

def main():
    while True:
        n = int(input().strip())
        if n == 0:
            break
            
        spheres = []
        for _ in range(n):
            x, y, z, r = map(int, input().split())
            spheres.append((x, y, z, r))
            
        m, binary = solve_dataset(spheres)
        print(m)
        print(binary)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

Wait, I see the issue - I'm missing the touching points between circles. Let me fix this with a proper implementation:

```python
import math

def distance_squared_2d(x1, y1, x2, y2):
    return (x1 - x2) ** 2 + (y1 - y2) ** 2

def distance_2d(x1, y1, x2, y2):
    return math.sqrt((x1 - x2) ** 2 + (y1 - y2) ** 2)

def find_touching_points(spheres):
    critical_z = set()
    
    # Add sphere boundaries
    for x, y, z, r in spheres:
        critical_z.add(z - r)
        critical_z.add(z + r)
    
    # Add touching points between spheres
    n = len(spheres)
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(i + 1, n):
            x1, y1, z1, r1 = spheres[i]
            x2, y2, z2, r2 = spheres[j]
            
            # Distance in xy-plane
            d_xy = distance_2d(x1, y1, x2, y2)
            
            # For circles to intersect in cross-section, need valid z
            if d_xy > r1 + r2 or d_xy < abs(r1 - r2):
                continue
                
            # Find z where r1z + r2z = d_xy, where r1z = sqrt(r1^2 - (z-z1)^2)
            # This gives sqrt(r1^2 - (z-z1)^2) + sqrt(r2^2 - (z-z2)^2) = d_xy
            
            # Valid z range
            z_min = max(z1 - r1, z2 - r2)
            z_max = min(z1 + r1, z2 + r2)
            
            if z_min >= z_max:
                continue
                
            # Binary search for roots of f(z) = sqrt(r1^2 - (z-z1)^2) + sqrt(r2^2 - (z-z2)^2) - d_xy
            def f(z):
                if z < z_min or z > z_max:
                    return 1e6
                r1z = math.sqrt(r1*r1 - (z - z1)*(z - z1))
                r2z = math.sqrt(r2*r2 - (z - z2)*(z - z2))
                return r1z + r2z - d_xy
            
            # We'll find roots by checking for sign changes
            # There might be 0, 1, or 2 roots
            roots = []
            
            # Check at several points
            h = 0.1  # step size
            for z in [z_min + h*i for i in range(0, int((z_max - z_min)/h) + 1)]:
                if z < z_min or z > z_max:
                    continue
                val = f(z)
                if abs(val) < 1e-5:
                    roots.append(z)
                    break
                # Check for sign changes
                if i > 0 and val * f(z - h) < 0:
                    # Root in this interval
                    # Use bisection to find it
                    left, right = z - h, z
                    while right - left > 1e-5:
                        mid = (left + right) / 2
                        if f(mid) * f(left) < 0:
                            right = mid
                        else:
                            left = mid
                    roots.append((left + right) / 2)
            
            # Add the found roots
            for root in roots:
                if root not in critical_z:
                    critical_z.add(root)
    
    return sorted(critical_z)

def solve_dataset(spheres):
    # Get all critical z-values
    critical_z = find_touching_points(spheres)
    
    # Calculate the state (number of connected components) at each critical z
    states = []
    
    for z in critical_z:
        # Find all circles at this height
        circles = []
        for x, y, z_center, r in spheres:
            if z >= z_center - r and z <= z_center + r:
                radius = math.sqrt(r*r - (z - z_center)*(z - z_center))
                circles.append((x, y, radius))
        
        # Calculate connected components
        if not circles:
            states.append(0)
            continue
            
        # Build graph where edges exist if circles touch
        n = len(circles)
        graph = [[] for _ in range(n)]
        
        for i in range(n):
            for j in range(i + 1, n):
                x1, y1, r1 = circles[i]
                x2, y2, r2 = circles[j]
                dist = distance_2d(x1, y1, x2, y2)
                if dist <= r1 + r2:
                    graph[i].append(j)
                    graph[j].append(i)
        
        # Count connected components using DFS
        visited = [False] * n
        components = 0
        
        for i in range(n):
            if not visited[i]:
                components += 1
                stack = [i]
                while stack:
                    node = stack.pop()
                    if visited[node]:
                        continue
                    visited[node] = True
                    for neighbor in graph[node]:
                        if not visited[neighbor]:
                            stack.append(neighbor)
        
        states.append(components)
    
    # Detect transitions
    if len(states) <= 1:
        return 0, "0"
    
    transitions = []
    for i in range(1, len(states)):
        if states[i] > states[i-1]:
            transitions.append('1')
        else:
            transitions.append('0')
    
    return len(transitions), ''.join(transitions)

def main():
    while True:
        line = input().strip()
        if line == '0':
            break
            
        n = int(line)
        spheres = []
        for _ in range(n):
            data = list(map(int, input().split()))
            spheres.append((data[0], data[1], data[2], data[3]))
            
        m, binary = solve_dataset(spheres)
        print(m)
        print(binary)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

In the year 2xxx, an expedition team landing on a planet found strange objects made by an ancient species living on that planet. They are transparent boxes containing opaque solid spheres (Figure 1). There are also many lithographs which seem to contain positions and radiuses of spheres.

<image>

Figure 1: A strange object

Initially their objective was unknown, but Professor Zambendorf found the cross section formed by a horizontal plane plays an important role. For example, the cross section of an object changes as in Figure 2 by sliding the plane from bottom to top.

<image>

Figure 2: Cross sections at different positions

He eventually found that some information is expressed by the transition of the number of connected figures in the cross section, where each connected figure is a union of discs intersecting or touching each other, and each disc is a cross section of the corresponding solid sphere. For instance, in Figure 2, whose geometry is described in the first sample dataset later, the number of connected figures changes as 0, 1, 2, 1, 2, 3, 2, 1, and 0, at z = 0.0000, 162.0000, 167.0000, 173.0004, 185.0000, 191.9996, 198.0000, 203.0000, and 205.0000, respectively. By assigning 1 for increment and 0 for decrement, the transitions of this sequence can be expressed by an 8-bit binary number 11011000.

For helping further analysis, write a program to determine the transitions when sliding the horizontal plane from bottom (z = 0) to top (z = 36000).



Input

The input consists of a series of datasets. Each dataset begins with a line containing a positive integer, which indicates the number of spheres N in the dataset. It is followed by N lines describing the centers and radiuses of the spheres. Each of the N lines has four positive integers Xi, Yi, Zi, and Ri (i = 1, . . . , N) describing the center and the radius of the i-th sphere, respectively.

You may assume 1 ≤ N ≤ 100, 1 ≤ Ri ≤ 2000, 0 < Xi - Ri < Xi + Ri < 4000, 0 < Yi - Ri < Yi + Ri < 16000, and 0 < Zi - Ri < Zi + Ri < 36000. Each solid sphere is defined as the set of all points (x, y, z) satisfying (x - Xi)2 + (y - Yi)2 + (z - Zi)2 ≤ Ri2.

A sphere may contain other spheres. No two spheres are mutually tangent. Every Zi ± Ri and minimum/maximum z coordinates of a circle formed by the intersection of any two spheres differ from each other by at least 0.01.

The end of the input is indicated by a line with one zero.

Output

For each dataset, your program should output two lines. The first line should contain an integer M indicating the number of transitions. The second line should contain an M-bit binary number that expresses the transitions of the number of connected figures as specified above.

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have transparent boxes containing solid spheres in 3D space
2. We're looking at horizontal cross-sections (constant z-plane) and analyzing connected components of discs
3. As we slide the plane from z=0 to z=36000, the number of connected components changes
4. We need to find the transition pattern (1 for increment, 0 for decrement) and output the binary number

## Key Insights

1. At each z-position, we have circles (cross-sections of spheres) in the xy-plane
2. Two circles are connected if they intersect or touch
3. The number of connected components changes at specific z-values (when circles touch or intersect)
4. We need to find all critical z-values where the number of connected components changes

## Solution Steps

1. **Find all critical z-values**: These are the z-coordinates where two spheres' cross-sections intersect or become tangent
2. **For each critical z-value**, determine the number of connected components
3. **Compare consecutive values** to determine if it's an increment (1) or decrement (0)
4. **Output the binary sequence**

## Detailed Algorithm

1. For each pair of spheres, find the z-values where their cross-sections intersect
2. The intersection occurs when: (x1-x2)² + (y1-y2)² + (z-z₀)² ≤ R₁² and (x1-x2)² + (y1-y2)² + (z-z₀)² ≤ R₂²
3. More precisely, for two spheres with centers (x₁,y₁,z₁,R₁) and (x₂,y₂,z₂,R₂), the cross-sections intersect when:
   - The distance between centers in xy-plane is d = √((x₁-x₂)² + (y₁-y₂)²)
   - If d > R₁ + R₂, no intersection
   - If d < |R₁ - R₂|, one sphere is inside other
   - Otherwise, they intersect in a range of z-values

4. The z-range for intersection can be calculated using the geometry of spheres

## Implementation

```python
import math

def solve():
    while True:
        n = int(input())
        if n == 0:
            break
            
        spheres = []
        for _ in range(n):
            x, y, z, r = map(int, input().split())
            spheres.append((x, y, z, r))
        
        # Find all critical z-values where the number of connected components changes
        critical_z = []
        
        # For each pair of spheres, find intersection z-values
        for i in range(n):
            for j in range(i + 1, n):
                x1, y1, z1, r1 = spheres[i]
                x2, y2, z2, r2 = spheres[j]
                
                # Distance in xy-plane
                dx = x1 - x2
                dy = y1 - y2
                d_xy = math.sqrt(dx * dx + dy * dy)
                
                # If spheres don't overlap in xy-plane
                if d_xy >= r1 + r2 or d_xy <= abs(r1 - r2):
                    # No intersection in xy-plane, but we still need to check if cross-sections touch
                    # Cross-sections are circles in xy-plane
                    # The intersection occurs when |z - z1| + |z - z2| is minimized
                    # Actually, we need to find when two circles at z intersect
                    # For two circles with centers at (x1,y1) and (x2,y2) and radii r1, r2
                    # They intersect when d_xy² ≤ (r1 + r2)² and d_xy² ≥ (r1 - r2)²
                    continue
                
                # Distance between centers
                distance = math.sqrt((x1 - x2)**2 + (y1 - y2)**2 + (z1 - z2)**2)
                
                # Calculate the z-values where the cross-sections intersect
                # Using the formula for intersection of two spheres
                # The cross-sections intersect when the distance between centers projected onto z-axis
                # and the xy-distance satisfy certain conditions
                
                # For the cross-sections to intersect, the condition is:
                # ||(x1-x2, y1-y2, z1-z2)||² = (r1 + r2)² or (r1 - r2)²
                # But for the z-coordinate, we need to find where the circles intersect
                
                # The circles intersect in the xy-plane at some z if:
                # (x1-x2)² + (y1-y2)² + (z-z1)² ≤ r1² and (x1-x2)² + (y1-y2)² + (z-z1)² ≤ r2²
                # Actually, for a fixed z, we have two circles in xy-plane
                # Circle 1: (x-x1)² + (y-y1)² ≤ r1² - (z-z1)²
                # Circle 2: (x-x2)² + (y-y2)² ≤ r2² - (z-z2)²
                
                # For intersection to happen, we need r1² - (z-z1)² > 0 and r2² - (z-z2)² > 0
                # So z must be in [z1-r1, z1+r1] and [z2-r2, z2+r2]
                
                # The condition for circles to intersect is that the distance between centers
                # in xy-plane plus the distance in z-direction is less than or equal to r1 + r2
                
                # Actually, we need to find the z-values where the two circles intersect
                # This happens when the distance between centers in 3D is less than or equal to r1 + r2
                # and greater than or equal to |r1 - r2|
                
                # Calculate the intersection of the z-ranges
                z1_min, z1_max = z1 - r1, z1 + r1
                z2_min, z2_max = z2 - r2, z2 + r2
                
                if z1_min > z2_max or z1_max < z2_min:
                    continue
                    
                # The critical z-values are where circles touch (tangency)
                # For two spheres, tangency occurs when distance = r1 + r2 or distance = |r1 - r2|
                
                # In 3D, the distance between centers is sqrt(d_xy² + (z - z_center)²)
                # But we need to find z such that distance = r1 + r2 and distance = |r1 - r2|
                
                # We need to solve: d_xy² + (z - z_c)² = (r1 + r2)² and d_xy² + (z - z_c)² = (r1 - r2)²
                
                # Actually, the distance between centers is: d^2 = d_xy² + (z - z_c)²
                # For tangency: d = r1 + r2 or d = |r1 - r2|
                
                # So we have: d_xy² + (z - z_c)² = (r1 + r2)²
                # and d_xy² + (z - z_c)² = (r1 - r2)²
                
                # But z_c is not defined. We need to solve for z in the equation:
                # d_xy² + (z - z1)² + (z - z2)² = (r1 + r2)²
                # d_xy² + (z - z1)² + (z - z2)² = (r1 - r2)²
                
                # Actually, the distance between centers squared is:
                # (x1-x2)² + (y1-y2)² + (z1-z)² + (z2-z)² = (r1 + r2)²
                
                # Let me correct this: the distance between two points (x1,y1,z) and (x2,y2,z) is
                # sqrt((x1-x2)² + (y1-y2)² + (z-z1)² + (z-z2)²)
                
                # Actually, we're fixing z, so the distance between centers at height z is:
                # d² = (x1-x2)² + (y1-y2)² + (z-z1)² + (z-z2)²
                
                # For tangency: d = r1 + r2 or d = |r1 - r2|
                
                # Solve d² = (r1 + r2)² and d² = (r1 - r2)²
                
                # Case 1: tangency with r1 + r2
                d_xy_sq = (x1 - x2) ** 2 + (y1 - y2) ** 2
                r_sum_sq = (r1 + r2) ** 2
                r_diff_sq = (r1 - r2) ** 2
                
                # For tangency case: d_xy² + (z - z1)² + (z - z2)² = (r1 + r2)²
                # (z - z1)² + (z - z2)² = (r1 + r2)² - d_xy²
                
                rhs_sum = r_sum_sq - d_xy_sq
                rhs_diff = r_diff_sq - d_xy_sq
                
                if rhs_sum > 0:
                    # Solve for z: (z - z1)² + (z - z2)² = rhs_sum
                    # z² - 2z(z1+z2) + z1² + z2² + 2z(z1+z2) - 2z(z1+z2) + 2z(z1+z2) = rhs_sum
                    # (z - z1)² + (z - z2)² = z² - 2z*z1 + z1² + z² - 2z*z2 + z2²
                    # = 2z² - 2z(z1+z2) + (z1² + z2²)
                    # = 2z² - 2z(z1+z2) + (z1² + z2²)
                    
                    a = 2
                    b = -2 * (z1 + z2)
                    c = z1*z1 + z2*z2 - rhs_sum
                    
                    # Solve quadratic: a*z² + b*z + c = 0
                    discriminant = b*b - 4*a*c
                    if discriminant >= 0:
                        sqrt_d = math.sqrt(discriminant)
                        z1_val = (-b + sqrt_d) / (2*a)
                        z2_val = (-b - sqrt_d) / (2*a)
                        if z1_val <= 36000 and z1_val >= 0:
                            critical_z.append(z1_val)
                        if z2_val <= 36000 and z2_val >= 0:
                            critical_z.append(z2_val)
                
                # Case 2: tangency with |r1 - r2|
                if rhs_diff > 0:
                    a = 2
                    b = -2 * (z1 + z2)
                    c = z1*z1 + z2*z2 - rhs_diff
                    
                    discriminant = b*b - 4*a*c
                    if discriminant >= 0:
                        sqrt_d = math.sqrt(discriminant)
                        z1_val = (-b + sqrt_d) / (2*a)
                        z2_val = (-b - sqrt_d) / (2*a)
                        if z1_val <= 36000 and z1_val >= 0:
                            critical_z.append(z1_val)
                        if z2_val <= 36000 and z2_val >= 0:
                            critical_z.append(z2_val)
        
        # Remove duplicates and sort
        critical_z = sorted(set(critical_z))
        
        # Also add boundaries z=0 and z=36000
        critical_z = [0.0] + critical_z + [36000.0]
        
        # Remove duplicates
        critical_z = sorted(set(critical_z))
        
        # Function to compute number of connected components at a given z
        def get_connected_components(z):
            if z < 0 or z > 36000:
                return 0
                
            # For each sphere, get the radius at height z
            circles = []
            for x, y, z_c, r in spheres:
                # Radius at height z
                if abs(z - z_c) > r:
                    # No intersection
                    continue
                radius = math.sqrt(r*r - (z - z_c)*(z - z_c))
                if radius > 0:
                    circles.append((x, y, radius))
            
            # Find connected components of circles in xy-plane
            # Two circles connect if they intersect
            if not circles:
                return 0
                
            # First, create points representing circle centers
            centers = [(x, y) for x, y, r in circles]
            radii = [r for x, y, r in circles]
            
            # Use union-find or simply check connectivity
            # For simplicity, check if each pair of circles intersects
            n_circles = len(circles)
            if n_circles == 0:
                return 0
                
            # For each circle, determine if it connects to others
            # Two circles intersect if distance between centers <= sum of radii
            connected = [False] * n_circles
            visited = [False] * n_circles
            
            # Count connected components
            components = 0
            for i in range(n_circles):
                if not visited[i]:
                    components += 1
                    # DFS/BFS to mark all connected circles
                    stack = [i]
                    visited[i] = True
                    while stack:
                        curr = stack.pop()
                        for j in range(n_circles):
                            if not visited[j]:
                                # Distance between centers
                                dx = centers[curr][0] - centers[j][0]
                                dy = centers[curr][1] - centers[j][1]
                                dist = math.sqrt(dx*dx + dy*dy)
                                # Circles intersect if distance <= sum of radii
                                if dist <= radii[curr] + radii[j]:
                                    visited[j] = True
                                    stack.append(j)
            
            return components
        
        # Compute transitions
        transitions = []
        for i in range(1, len(critical_z)):
            prev_components = get_connected_components(critical_z[i-1])
            curr_components = get_connected_components(critical_z[i])
            
            if curr_components > prev_components:
                transitions.append('1')
            elif curr_components < prev_components:
                transitions.append('0')
            else:
                # Equal, no transition
                continue
        
        # If no transitions, output 0 and 0
        if not transitions:
            print(0)
            print("0")
        else:
            print(len(transitions))
            print(''.join(transitions))
```

```python
        def count_components(z):

            circles = []
```

You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

In the year 2xxx, an expedition team landing on a planet found strange objects made by an ancient species living on that planet. They are transparent boxes containing opaque solid spheres (Figure 1). There are also many lithographs which seem to contain positions and radiuses of spheres.

<image>

Figure 1: A strange object

Initially their objective was unknown, but Professor Zambendorf found the cross section formed by a horizontal plane plays an important role. For example, the cross section of an object changes as in Figure 2 by sliding the plane from bottom to top.

<image>

Figure 2: Cross sections at different positions

He eventually found that some information is expressed by the transition of the number of connected figures in the cross section, where each connected figure is a union of discs intersecting or touching each other, and each disc is a cross section of the corresponding solid sphere. For instance, in Figure 2, whose geometry is described in the first sample dataset later, the number of connected figures changes as 0, 1, 2, 1, 2, 3, 2, 1, and 0, at z = 0.0000, 162.0000, 167.0000, 173.0004, 185.0000, 191.9996, 198.0000, 203.0000, and 205.0000, respectively. By assigning 1 for increment and 0 for decrement, the transitions of this sequence can be expressed by an 8-bit binary number 11011000.

For helping further analysis, write a program to determine the transitions when sliding the horizontal plane from bottom (z = 0) to top (z = 36000).



Input

The input consists of a series of datasets. Each dataset begins with a line containing a positive integer, which indicates the number of spheres N in the dataset. It is followed by N lines describing the centers and radiuses of the spheres. Each of the N lines has four positive integers Xi, Yi, Zi, and Ri (i = 1, . . . , N) describing the center and the radius of the i-th sphere, respectively.

You may assume 1 ≤ N ≤ 100, 1 ≤ Ri ≤ 2000, 0 < Xi - Ri < Xi + Ri < 4000, 0 < Yi - Ri < Yi + Ri < 16000, and 0 < Zi - Ri < Zi + Ri < 36000. Each solid sphere is defined as the set of all points (x, y, z) satisfying (x - Xi)2 + (y - Yi)2 + (z - Zi)2 ≤ Ri2.

A sphere may contain other spheres. No two spheres are mutually tangent. Every Zi ± Ri and minimum/maximum z coordinates of a circle formed by the intersection of any two spheres differ from each other by at least 0.01.

The end of the input is indicated by a line with one zero.

Output

For each dataset, your program should output two lines. The first line should contain an integer M indicating the number of transitions. The second line should contain an M-bit binary number that expresses the transitions of the number of connected figures as specified above.

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have transparent boxes containing opaque solid spheres in 3D space
2. We're looking at horizontal cross-sections at different z-levels (from z=0 to z=36000)
3. Each cross-section at height z shows circles (cross-sections of spheres) on a 2D plane
4. Connected figures are groups of circles that touch or intersect
5. We need to find when the number of connected components changes and record the transitions

## Key Insights

1. At height z, a sphere contributes a circle with radius r = √(R² - (z-Zi)²) if |z-Zi| ≤ Ri
2. The number of connected components changes only at specific z-values where:
   - A new sphere becomes visible (z = Zi - Ri)
   - An existing sphere disappears (z = Zi + Ri)
   - Two circles touch or intersect (crossing point)

3. For two circles to touch, we need to find when the distance between centers equals the sum or difference of radii.

## Step-by-Step Solution

### Step 1: Identify all critical z-values
- When a sphere starts (z = Zi - Ri)
- When a sphere ends (z = Zi + Ri)
- When two spheres' circles touch (distance between centers = sum or difference of radii)

### Step 2: For each critical z-value, calculate the number of connected components
- For each z, find all visible circles (where z is in [Zi-Ri, Zi+Ri])
- Group circles that are connected (intersect or touch)
- Count connected components

### Step 3: Determine transitions
- Track how the number of components changes
- 1 for increment, 0 for decrement

```python
import math
from collections import defaultdict

def distance_squared(p1, p2):
    return (p1[0] - p2[0])**2 + (p1[1] - p2[1])**2

def circles_touch(c1, c2):
    # c1 and c2 are (x, y, r) tuples
    d_sq = distance_squared(c1[:2], c2[:2])
    r1, r2 = c1[2], c2[2]
    
    # Two circles touch if distance between centers = sum or absolute difference of radii
    d = math.sqrt(d_sq)
    
    # Check if they touch externally or internally
    return abs(r1 - r2) <= d <= r1 + r2

def get_connected_components(circles):
    if not circles:
        return 0
    
    n = len(circles)
    if n == 1:
        return 1
    
    # Use union-find to group connected circles
    parent = list(range(n))
    
    def find(x):
        if parent[x] != x:
            parent[x] = find(parent[x])
        return parent[x]
    
    def union(x, y):
        px, py = find(x), find(y)
        if px != py:
            parent[px] = py
    
    # Check all pairs of circles
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(i + 1, n):
            if circles_touch(circles[i], circles[j]):
                union(i, j)
    
    # Count unique roots
    roots = set()
    for i in range(n):
        roots.add(find(i))
    
    return len(roots)

def solve_dataset(spheres):
    n = len(spheres)
    
    # Collect all critical z-values
    critical_z = []
    
    # Add start and end points for each sphere
    for x, y, z, r in spheres:
        start_z = z - r
        end_z = z + r
        critical_z.append(start_z)
        critical_z.append(end_z)
    
    # Add intersection points between any two spheres
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(i + 1, n):
            x1, y1, z1, r1 = spheres[i]
            x2, y2, z2, r2 = spheres[j]
            
            # Distance between centers in xy-plane
            dist_xy = math.sqrt((x1 - x2)**2 + (y1 - y2)**2)
            
            # For circles to touch at height z:
            # (z - z1)^2 + (z - z2)^2 + dist_xy^2 = (r1^2 - (z-z1)^2) + (r2^2 - (z-z2)^2)
            # This is complex, so we'll check all critical points
            
            # We need to find z such that the circles touch
            # The distance between centers in 3D is sqrt(dist_xy^2 + (z-z1)^2 + (z-z2)^2)
            # The sum of radii is r1 + r2
            # The absolute difference of radii is |r1 - r2|
            
            # For touch: sqrt(dist_xy^2 + (z-z1)^2 + (z-z2)^2) = r1 + r2 or |r1 - r2|
            
            # We solve for z in both cases
            # Case 1: distance = r1 + r2
            # Case 2: distance = |r1 - r2|
            
            # This is a quadratic equation in z
            # But we can find the critical z values by considering when the circles reach touch
            
            # Actually, since the problem states that all critical points differ by at least 0.01,
            # we can just check all critical z values and look for intersections
            
            # We add the critical z values from sphere boundaries
            # For intersection, we'll add the calculated z values
            
            # For simplify, since the problem states all critical points differ by at least 0.01,
            # we can just work with the sphere boundaries first
            
            # But we need to add intersection points
            # Solving the equation:
            # dist_xy^2 + (z-z1)^2 + (z-z2)^2 = (r1+r2)^2
            # dist_xy^2 + (z-z1)^2 + (z-z2)^2 = (r1-r2)^2
            
            # Case 1: sum of radii
            # (z-z1)^2 + (z-z2)^2 = (r1+r2)^2 - dist_xy^2
            # Let d1 = z-z1, d2 = z-z2
            # d1^2 + d2^2 = constant
            # We need to solve this quadratic
            
            pass
    
    # Actually, let's simplify - this is a known problem about detecting when circles intersect
    # We'll use the fact that the only time the number of components changes is at sphere boundaries
    # and intersection points of circles
    
    # But given the complexity, let's implement a more straightforward approach
    
    critical_z = []
    
    # Add all sphere boundaries
    for x, y, z, r in spheres:
        critical_z.append(z - r)
        critical_z.append(z + r)
    
    # Remove duplicates and sort
    critical_z = sorted(set(critical_z))
    
    # For each pair of spheres, find when their cross-sections touch
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(i + 1, n):
            x1, y1, z1, r1 = spheres[i]
            x2, y2, z2, r2 = spheres[j]
            
            # Distance in xy plane
            d_xy = math.sqrt((x1 - x2)**2 + (y1 - y2)**2)
            
            # The circles touch when the distance between centers equals sum or difference of radii
            # In 3D, the distance between centers at height z is sqrt(d_xy^2 + (z-z1)^2 + (z-z2)^2)
            
            # Case 1: external touch (distance = r1 + r2)
            # sqrt(d_xy^2 + (z-z1)^2 + (z-z2)^2) = r1 + r2
            # d_xy^2 + (z-z1)^2 + (z-z2)^2 = (r1+r2)^2
            
            # Let A = (z-z1)^2 + (z-z2)^2 = (r1+r2)^2 - d_xy^2
            # This is a quadratic: 2z^2 - 2(z1+z2)z + (z1^2 + z2^2) = A
            # 2z^2 - 2(z1+z2)z + (z1^2 + z2^2 - A) = 0
            
            A_val = (r1 + r2) ** 2 - d_xy ** 2
            if A_val >= 0:
                # Solve quadratic: 2z^2 - 2(z1+z2)z + (z1^2 + z2^2 - A_val) = 0
                a = 2
                b = -2 * (z1 + z2)
                c = z1**2 + z2**2 - A_val
                
                discriminant = b**2 - 4*a*c
                if discriminant >= 0:
                    z1_sol = (-b + math.sqrt(discriminant)) / (2*a)
                    z2_sol = (-b - math.sqrt(discriminant)) / (2*a)
                    critical_z.append(z1_sol)
                    critical_z.append(z2_sol)
            
            # Case 2: internal touch (distance = |r1 - r2|)
            # sqrt(d_xy^2 + (z-z1)^2 + (z-z2)^2) = |r1 - r2|
            B_val = (r1 - r2) ** 2 - d_xy ** 2
            if B_val >= 0:
                # Solve: (z-z1)^2 + (z-z2)^2 = B_val
                a = 2
                b = -2 * (z1 + z2)
                c = z1**2 + z2**2 - B_val
                
                discriminant = b**2 - 4*a*c
                if discriminant >= 0:
                    z1_sol = (-b + math.sqrt(discriminant)) / (2*a)
                    z2_sol = (-b - math.sqrt(discriminant)) / (2*a)
                    critical_z.append(z1_sol)
                    critical_z.append(z2_sol)
    
    # Remove duplicates and sort
    critical_z = sorted(set(critical_z))
    
    # Add boundaries 0 and 36000
    critical_z = [0.0] + critical_z + [36000.0]
    
    # Remove duplicates
    critical_z = sorted(set(critical_z))
    
    # If first and last are same, we need to ensure we have at least one point
    if len(critical_z) == 1:
        critical_z = [0.0, 36000.0]
    
    # Now compute the number of connected components at each critical z
    components = []
    for z in critical_z:
        # Find all visible circles at height z
        visible_circles = []
        for x, y, z_center, r in spheres:
            # Check if z is in [z_center - r, z_center + r]
            if z >= z_center - r and z <= z_center + r:
                # Calculate radius at height z
                h = z - z_center
                r_at_z = math.sqrt(r*r - h*h)
                visible_circles.append((x, y, r_at_z))
        
        # Count connected components
        if not visible_circles:
            components.append(0)
        else:
            comp = get_connected_components(visible_circles)
            components.append(comp)
    
    # Now determine the transitions
    transitions = []
    for i in range(1, len(components)):
        if components[i] > components[i-1]:
            transitions.append('1')
        elif components[i] < components[i-1]:
            transitions.append('0')
        else:
            # Same component count, no transition
            transitions.append('0')  # or should we skip?
    
    # But wait - we might have missed some transitions
    # Actually, we need to check when the count increases or decreases
    
    # Actually, the problem says to assign 1 for increment and 0 for decrement
    # So we just need to capture when it increases or decreases
    
    # Let's recode the transition detection
    transition_bits = []
    for i in range(1, len(components)):
        if components[i] > components[i-1]:
            transition_bits.append('1')
        elif components[i] < components[i-1]:
            transition_bits.append('0')
        else:
            # No change, so no transition (bit 0)
            transition_bits.append('0')
    
    # But the example shows 8 transitions for the first dataset
    # Let's verify if we need to include all changes
    
    # The problem says "the transitions of the number of connected figures"
    # And assigns 1 for increment, 0 for decrement
    
    return len(transition_bits), ''.join(transition_bits)

# Main program
while True:
    n = int(input().strip())
    if n == 0:
        break
    
    spheres = []
    for _ in range(n):
        x, y, z, r = map(int, input().split())
        spheres.append((x, y, z, r))
    
    # Solve the dataset
    m, binary = solve_dataset(spheres)
    
    print(m)
    print(binary)
```

```python
import math
from collections import defaultdict

def distance_squared(p1, p2):
    return (p1[0] - p2[0])**2 + (p1[1] - p2[1])**2

def circles_touch(c1, c2):
    # c1 and c2 are (x, y, r) tuples
    d_sq = distance_squared(c1[:2], c2[:2])
    r1, r2 = c1[2], c2[2]
    
    # Two circles touch if distance between centers = sum or absolute difference of radii
    d = math.sqrt(d_sq)
    
    # For circles to be connected, they must touch or intersect
    # They intersect if distance <= sum of radii and distance >= |r1 - r2|
    return d <= r1 + r2 and d >= abs(r1 - r2)

def get_connected_components(circles):
    if not circles:
        return 0
    
    n = len(circles)
    if n == 1:
        return 1
    
    # Use union-find to group connected circles
    parent = list(range(n))
    
    def find(x):
        if parent[x] != x:
            parent[x] = find(parent[x])
        return parent[x]
    
    def union(x, y):
        px, py = find(x), find(y)
        if px != py:
            parent[px] = py
    
    # Check all pairs of circles
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(i + 1, n):
            if circles_touch(circles[i], circles[j]):
                union(i, j)
    
    # Count unique roots
    roots = set()
    for i in range(n):
        roots.add(find(i))
    
    return len(roots)

def solve_dataset(spheres):
    n = len(spheres)
    
    # Collect all critical z-values (where circles appear/disappear or touch)
    critical_z = []
    
    # Add start and end points for each sphere
    for x, y, z, r in spheres:
        start_z = z - r
        end_z = z + r
        critical_z.append(start_z)
        critical_z.append(end_z)
    
    # Add intersection points between any two spheres
    for i in range(n):

            x2, y2, z2, r2 = spheres[j]
            
            # Distance in xy plane
            d_xy = math.sqrt((x1 - x2)**2 + (y1 - y2)**2)
            
            # For circles to touch at height z:
            # The 3D distance between centers = sqrt(d_xy^2 + (z-z1)^2 + (z-z2)^2)
            # This equals r1 + r2 (external touch) or |r1 - r2| (internal touch)
            
            # Case 1: external touch
            sum_radii = r1 + r2
            if d_xy < sum_radii:  # Possible touch
                # We have: d_xy^2 + (z-z1)^2 + (z-z2)^2 = sum_radii^2
                # (z-z1)^2 + (z-z2)^2 = sum_radii^2 - d_xy^2
                right_side = sum_radii**2 - d_xy**2
                
                if right_side >= 0:
                    # This is a quadratic equation: 2z^2 - 2(z1+z2)z + (z1^2 + z2^2) = right_side
                    a = 2
                    b = -2 * (z1 + z2)
                    c = z1**2 + z2**2 - right_side
                    
                    discriminant = b**2 - 4*a*c
                    if discriminant >= 0:
                        z1_sol = (-b + math.sqrt(discriminant)) / (2*a)
                        z2_sol = (-b - math.sqrt(discriminant)) / (2*a)
                        critical_z.append(z1_sol)
                        critical_z.append(z2_sol)
            
            # Case 2: internal touch
            diff_radii = abs(r1 - r2)
            if d_xy < diff_radii:  # Possible touch
                # We have: d_xy^2 + (z-z1)^2 + (z-z2)^2 = diff_radii^2
                right_side = diff_radii**2 - d_xy**2
                
                if right_side >= 0:
                    # This is a quadratic equation: 2z^2 - 2(z1+z2)z + (z1^2 + z2^2) = right_side
                    a = 2
                    b = -2 * (z1 + z2)

                    if discriminant >= 0:

                        z2_sol = (-b - math.sqrt(discriminant)) / (2*a)
                        critical_z.append(z1_sol)
                        critical_z.append(z2_sol)
    
    # Sort and remove duplicates
    critical_z = sorted(set(critical_z))
    
    # Add boundaries
    critical_z = [0.0] + critical_z + [36000.0]
    critical_z = sorted(set(critical_z))
    
    # Calculate number of connected components at each critical z
    components = []
    for z in critical_z:
        # Find all visible circles at height z
        visible_circles = []
        for x, y, z_center, r in spheres:
            # Check if z is in [z_center - r, z_center + r]
            if z >= z_center - r and z <= z_center + r:
                # Calculate radius at height z
                h = z - z_center
                r_at_z = math.sqrt(r*r - h*h)
                visible_circles.append((x, y, r_at_z))
        
        # Count connected components
        if not visible_circles:
            components.append(0)
        else:
            comp = get_connected_components(visible_circles)
            components.append(comp)
    
    # Determine transitions: 1 for increment, 0 for decrement
    transitions = []
    for i in range(1, len(components)):
        if components[i] > components[i-1]:
            transitions.append('1')
        elif components[i] < components[i-1]:
            transitions.append('0')
        else:
            # No change, so no transition
            transitions.append('0')
    
    return len(transitions), ''.join(transitions)
```

import math
from collections import defaultdict

def distance_squared(p1, p2):
    return (p1[0] - p2[0])**2 + (p1[1] - p2[1])**2

def circles_touch(c1, c2):
    # c1 and c2 are (x, y, r) tuples
    d_sq = distance_squared(c1[:2], c2[:2])
    r1, r2 = c1[2], c2[2]
    
    # Two circles touch if distance between centers = sum or absolute difference of radii
    d = math.sqrt(d_sq)
    
    # For circles to be connected, they must touch or intersect
    # They intersect if distance <= sum of radii and distance >= |r1 - r2|
    return d <= r1 + r2 and d >= abs(r1 - r2)

def get_connected_components(circles):
    if not circles:
        return 0
    
    n = len(circles)
    if n == 1:
        return 1
    
    # Use union-find to group connected circles
    parent = list(range(n))
    
    def find(x):
        if parent[x] != x:
            parent[x] = find(parent[x])
        return parent[x]
    
    def union(x, y):
        px, py = find(x), find(y)
        if px != py:
            parent[px] = py
    
    # Check all pairs of circles
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(i + 1, n):
            if circles_touch(circles[i], circles[j]):
                union(i, j)
    
    # Count unique roots
    roots = set()
    for i in range(n):
        roots.add(find(i))
    
    return len(roots)

                    a = 2
                    b = -2 * (z1 + z2)

                    discriminant = b**2 - 4*a*c
                    if discriminant >= 0:
                        z1_sol = (-b + math.sqrt(discriminant)) / (2*a)
                        z2_sol = (-b - math.sqrt(discriminant)) / (2*a)

            if z >= z_center - r and z <= z_center + r:
                # Calculate radius at height z
                h = z - z_center
                r_at_z = math.sqrt(r*r - h*h)
                visible_circles.append((x, y, r_at_z))
        
        # Count connected components
        if not visible_circles:
            components.append(0)
        else:
            comp = get_connected_components(visible_circles)
            components.append(comp)
    
    # Determine transitions: 1 for increment, 0 for decrement
    transitions = []
    for i in range(1, len(components)):
        if components[i] > components[i-1]:
            transitions.append('1')
        elif components[i] < components[i-1]:
            transitions.append('0')
        else:
            # No change, so no transition
            transitions.append('0')
    
    return len(transitions), ''.join(transitions)

# Main program
while True:
    n = int(input().strip())
    if n == 0:
        break
    
    spheres = []
    for _ in range(n):
        x, y, z, r = map(int, input().split())
        spheres.append((x, y, z, r))
    
    m, binary = solve_dataset(spheres)
    
    print(m)
    print(binary)
